└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────┴─────────────┴───────────┘

$$\text{Final Percentage} = \frac{\text{Total Earned Points}}{\text{Total Possible Points}} \times 100$$

  • Passing Threshold: 66.0%
  • Recommended Candidate Target: 80.0%+ (provides a safe buffer for PSI remote desktop latency).

2. SECONDARY COMPETENCY METRICS

Evaluation Dimension Benchmark Target Remediation Action if Below Benchmark
Command-Line Efficiency $\le 30$ seconds to generate any boilerplate YAML via --dry-run=client -o yaml. Practice Section 74 speed setup drills daily.
Context Switching Hygiene 100% compliance; zero tasks executed in the wrong cluster context. Tape a note to monitor: "CHECK CONTEXT FIRST".
Time Management Complete 15 tasks within the first 90 minutes; 30 minutes for pass 3. Enforce the 3-Pass Strategy (Pass 1: quick wins).
Troubleshooting Speed Diagnose a NotReady node or crashed static pod in $< 8$ minutes. Review Volume III diagnostic decision trees.

3. AUTOMATED VERIFICATION SCRIPT TEMPLATE

Candidates running local test clusters can execute this bash assertion script to automatically verify task completion:

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Automated Verification Harness for CKA Mock Exam 4
set -uo pipefail

SCORE=0
MAX_SCORE=100

echo "=================================================="
echo "STARTING AUTOMATED VERIFICATION AUDIT"
echo "=================================================="

# Check Task 1: Node Ready
if kubectl get nodes node-2 | grep -q "Ready"; then
    echo "[PASS] Task 1: node-2 is Ready (+6)"
    SCORE=$((SCORE + 6))
else
    echo "[FAIL] Task 1: node-2 is NotReady"
fi

# Check Task 8: etcd snapshot exists
if [ -f /var/data/etcd-final-mock.db ]; then
    echo "[PASS] Task 8: etcd snapshot exists (+6)"
    SCORE=$((SCORE + 6))
else
    echo "[FAIL] Task 8: etcd snapshot missing"
fi

# Check Task 9: ServiceAccount & Role
if kubectl auth can-i create deployments -n operations --as=system:serviceaccount:operations:deployer | grep -q "yes"; then
    echo "[PASS] Task 9: ServiceAccount permissions verified (+4)"
    SCORE=$((SCORE + 4))
else
    echo "[FAIL] Task 9: ServiceAccount cannot create deployments"
fi

# Check Task 19: PV Bound
if kubectl get pvc pvc-storage-1 2>/dev/null | grep -q "Bound"; then
    echo "[PASS] Task 19: PVC pvc-storage-1 is Bound (+5)"
    SCORE=$((SCORE + 5))
else
    echo "[FAIL] Task 19: PVC is not Bound"
fi

echo "=================================================="
echo "TOTAL SCORE: ${SCORE} / ${MAX_SCORE}"
if [ $SCORE -ge 66 ]; then
    echo "STATUS: CANDIDATE PASSED"
else
    echo "STATUS: CANDIDATE FAILED (Threshold: 66)"
fi
echo "=================================================="

VOLUME V: THE CKA MOCK EXAMINATION SYSTEM

MOCK EXAM 1: BEGINNER TO INTERMEDIATE SIMULATION

EXAMINATION SPECIFICATIONS

  • Target Difficulty: Beginner to Intermediate
  • Time Allowed: 120 minutes (2.0 hours)
  • Passing Score: 66% (Total Possible: 100%)
  • Number of Tasks: 17 Performance-Based Tasks
  • Cluster Contexts: k8s, cluster2, wk8s

TASK 1: Pod Deployment and Label Filtering (Weight: 4%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace ingress-basic does not exist.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create namespace ingress-basic.
    2. Create a Pod named frontend-pod running image nginx:1.27 in namespace ingress-basic.
    3. Assign labels tier=frontend and app=web.
    4. Expose container port 80.
  • Expected Final Condition: Pod frontend-pod is Running in namespace ingress-basic with both labels.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get pod frontend-pod -n ingress-basic --show-labels
  • Solution:
    kubectl config use-context k8s
kubectl create namespace ingress-basic
kubectl run frontend-pod --image=nginx:1.27 --port=80 --labels="tier=frontend,app=web" -n ingress-basic

TASK 2: Multi-Container Pod with EmptyDir Sharing (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace default.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create Pod log-pipeline in namespace default.
    2. Container 1 named writer: image busybox:1.36, appends date to /var/log/app.log every 5 seconds.
    3. Container 2 named reader: image busybox:1.36, executes tail -f /var/log/app.log.
    4. Both containers must mount an emptyDir volume at /var/log.
  • Expected Final Condition: Both containers show 2/2 Running and reader prints timestamps.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl logs log-pipeline -c reader
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: log-pipeline
spec:
  volumes:
  - name: shared-vol
    emptyDir: {}
  containers:
  - name: writer
    image: busybox:1.36
    command: ["/bin/sh", "-c", "while true; do date >> /var/log/app.log; sleep 5; done"]
    volumeMounts:
    - name: shared-vol
      mountPath: /var/log
  - name: reader
    image: busybox:1.36
    command: ["/bin/sh", "-c", "tail -f /var/log/app.log"]
    volumeMounts:
    - name: shared-vol
      mountPath: /var/log

TASK 3: ServiceAccount and RBAC RoleBinding (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace security-zone.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create namespace security-zone.
    2. Create a ServiceAccount named operator-sa in security-zone.
    3. Create a Role named pod-admin allowing get, list, watch, and delete on pods.
    4. Bind pod-admin to operator-sa via RoleBinding operator-pod-bind.
  • Expected Final Condition: operator-sa can delete pods in security-zone but cannot delete deployments.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl auth can-i delete pods -n security-zone --as=system:serviceaccount:security-zone:operator-sa
  • Solution:
    kubectl create namespace security-zone
kubectl create serviceaccount operator-sa -n security-zone
kubectl create role pod-admin -n security-zone --verb=get,list,watch,delete --resource=pods
kubectl create rolebinding operator-pod-bind -n security-zone --role=pod-admin --serviceaccount=security-zone:operator-sa

TASK 4: Deployment Scaling and Rolling Update (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace default.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create Deployment micro-api running nginx:1.25 with 2 replicas.
    2. Scale the Deployment to 5 replicas.
    3. Update image to nginx:1.26 and annotate with change-cause: "Updated to 1.26".
    4. Verify all 5 replicas are updated.
  • Expected Final Condition: 5 replicas running image nginx:1.26 with change-cause recorded.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl rollout status deployment micro-api
kubectl rollout history deployment micro-api
  • Solution:
    kubectl create deployment micro-api --image=nginx:1.25 --replicas=2
kubectl scale deployment micro-api --replicas=5
kubectl set image deployment/micro-api nginx=nginx:1.26
kubectl annotate deployment/micro-api kubernetes.io/change-cause="Updated to 1.26" --overwrite

TASK 5: Secret Creation and Pod Environment Injection (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace data-tier.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create namespace data-tier.
    2. Create Secret db-passwords containing DB_PASS=AlpineP@ss2026.
    3. Deploy Pod db-runner (alpine, sleeping 3600) injecting DB_PASS as an environment variable from Secret db-passwords.
  • Expected Final Condition: kubectl exec db-runner -- env shows DB_PASS=AlpineP@ss2026.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl exec db-runner -n data-tier -- env | grep DB_PASS
  • Solution:
    kubectl create namespace data-tier
kubectl create secret generic db-passwords --from-literal=DB_PASS='AlpineP@ss2026' -n data-tier

    apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: db-runner
  namespace: data-tier
spec:
  containers:
  - name: runner
    image: alpine
    command: ["sleep", "3600"]
    env:
    - name: DB_PASS
      valueFrom:
        secretKeyRef:
          name: db-passwords
          key: DB_PASS

TASK 6: PersistentVolume & PersistentVolumeClaim Binding (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Host directory /data/local-vol.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create PersistentVolume pv-disk-1 with capacity 3Gi, accessMode ReadWriteOnce, hostPath /data/local-vol, and storageClassName local-disk.
    2. Create PersistentVolumeClaim pvc-disk-1 in default requesting 2Gi on storageClassName local-disk with accessMode ReadWriteOnce.
    3. Confirm the claim status is Bound.
  • Expected Final Condition: Both PV and PVC show status Bound.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get pvc pvc-disk-1
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolume
metadata:
  name: pv-disk-1
spec:
  capacity:
    storage: 3Gi
  accessModes:
    - ReadWriteOnce
  storageClassName: local-disk
  hostPath:
    path: /data/local-vol
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolumeClaim
metadata:
  name: pvc-disk-1
spec:
  storageClassName: local-disk
  accessModes:
    - ReadWriteOnce
  resources:
    requests:
      storage: 2Gi

TASK 7: ClusterIP Service and DNS Discovery (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Deployment api-server exists in production.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create a ClusterIP Service named api-svc in production exposing Deployment api-server on port 80 targeting port 8080.
    2. Verify that EndpointSlices are created and contain valid IPs.
  • Expected Final Condition: api-svc resolves internally and has active endpoints.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get endpoints api-svc -n production
  • Solution:
    kubectl expose deployment api-server --name=api-svc --port=80 --target-port=8080 -n production

TASK 8: Node Maintenance: Cordon and Safe Drain (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context cluster2
  • Initial Condition: Worker node cluster2-worker-1 is running workloads.
  • Task Description:
    1. Switch to context cluster2.
    2. Cordon cluster2-worker-1.
    3. Drain all workloads from cluster2-worker-1 safely, handling DaemonSets and emptyDir volumes.
  • Expected Final Condition: Node status shows Ready,SchedulingDisabled with 0 non-daemonset pods.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get node cluster2-worker-1
  • Solution:
    kubectl config use-context cluster2
kubectl cordon cluster2-worker-1
kubectl drain cluster2-worker-1 --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data --force

TASK 9: Pod Scheduling with NodeSelector (Weight: 4%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Worker nodes untagged.
  • Task Description:
    1. Label node k8s-worker-1 with disk=ssd.
    2. Deploy Pod cache-pod running redis:alpine with a nodeSelector requiring disk: ssd.
    3. Verify Pod lands on k8s-worker-1.
  • Expected Final Condition: cache-pod is Running on k8s-worker-1.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get pod cache-pod -o wide
  • Solution:
    kubectl config use-context k8s
kubectl label node k8s-worker-1 disk=ssd
kubectl run cache-pod --image=redis:alpine --dry-run=client -o yaml > cache.yaml
# Add nodeSelector: disk: ssd to spec
kubectl apply -f cache.yaml

TASK 10: Taints and Tolerations (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Node k8s-worker-2.
  • Task Description:
    1. Apply taint dedicated=gpu:NoSchedule to node k8s-worker-2.
    2. Deploy Pod gpu-app running alpine sleeping 3600 that tolerates dedicated=gpu:NoSchedule.
    3. Verify Pod schedules onto k8s-worker-2.
  • Expected Final Condition: Pod is running on k8s-worker-2.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get pod gpu-app -o wide
  • Solution:
    kubectl taint nodes k8s-worker-2 dedicated=gpu:NoSchedule

    apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: gpu-app
spec:
  nodeName: k8s-worker-2
  tolerations:
  - key: "dedicated"
    operator: "Equal"
    value: "gpu"
    effect: "NoSchedule"
  containers:
  - name: app
    image: alpine
    command: ["sleep", "3600"]

TASK 11: Ingress Resource Path Routing (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Ingress controller installed. Services auth-svc and pay-svc running on port 80.
  • Task Description:
    1. Author an Ingress gateway-ingress in default.
    2. IngressClass: nginx. Host: api.test.local.
    3. Path /auth $\to$ auth-svc:80.
    4. Path /pay $\to$ pay-svc:80.
  • Expected Final Condition: Ingress created with Prefix path matching.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl describe ingress gateway-ingress
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: Ingress
metadata:
  name: gateway-ingress
spec:
  ingressClassName: nginx
  rules:
  - host: api.test.local
    http:
      paths:
      - path: /auth
        pathType: Prefix
        backend:
          service:
            name: auth-svc
            port:
              number: 80
      - path: /pay
        pathType: Prefix
        backend:
          service:
            name: pay-svc
            port:
              number: 80

TASK 12: NetworkPolicy Restricting Ingress (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace secure-backend.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create NetworkPolicy isolate-backend in secure-backend selecting pods labeled role=db.
    2. Permit incoming TCP traffic on port 5432 strictly from pods labeled role=api.
  • Expected Final Condition: Non-api pods cannot reach port 5432.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl describe netpol isolate-backend -n secure-backend
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: isolate-backend
  namespace: secure-backend
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      role: db
  policyTypes:
  - Ingress
  ingress:
  - from:
    - podSelector:
        matchLabels:
          role: api
    ports:
    - protocol: TCP
      port: 5432

TASK 13: etcd Snapshot Backup (Weight: 8%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Control plane node.
  • Task Description:
    1. SSH to control plane.
    2. Save etcd snapshot to /opt/backup/etcd-mock1.db using client certificates in /etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/.
    3. Verify snapshot status.
  • Expected Final Condition: Snapshot file exists with non-zero size and valid revision.
  • Verification Command:
    sudo ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl --write-out=table snapshot status /opt/backup/etcd-mock1.db
  • Solution:
    sudo ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl \
  --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379 \
  --cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt \
  --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.crt \
  --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.key \
  snapshot save /opt/backup/etcd-mock1.db

TASK 14: Gateway API HTTPRoute Deployment (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Gateway shared-gw exists in infra. Service order-svc exists in sales.
  • Task Description:
    1. In namespace sales, create an HTTPRoute named order-route attaching to parent Gateway shared-gw in namespace infra.
    2. Route path prefix /orders to Service order-svc on port 80.
  • Expected Final Condition: order-route reports Accepted: True.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl describe httproute order-route -n sales
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: HTTPRoute
metadata:
  name: order-route
  namespace: sales
spec:
  parentRefs:
  - name: shared-gw
    namespace: infra
  rules:
  - matches:
    - path:
        type: PathPrefix
        value: /orders
    backendRefs:
    - name: order-svc
      port: 80

TASK 15: Pod Liveness and Readiness Probe Configuration (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace default.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create Pod probe-app running nginx:1.27.
    2. Add readiness probe: HTTP GET / on port 80, initialDelaySeconds 5.
    3. Add liveness probe: HTTP GET / on port 80, periodSeconds 10.
  • Expected Final Condition: Pod is 1/1 Running.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl describe pod probe-app | grep -E "Liveness|Readiness"
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: probe-app
spec:
  containers:
  - name: nginx
    image: nginx:1.27
    readinessProbe:
      httpGet:
        path: /
        port: 80
      initialDelaySeconds: 5
    livenessProbe:
      httpGet:
        path: /
        port: 80
      periodSeconds: 10

TASK 16: JSONPath Extraction of Node Internal IPs (Weight: 4%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Multi-node cluster.
  • Task Description:
    1. Extract the Internal IP of all nodes in the cluster using kubectl get nodes -o jsonpath=....
    2. Save the output string to /opt/node-ips.txt.
  • Expected Final Condition: /opt/node-ips.txt contains space-separated Internal IPs.
  • Verification Command:
    cat /opt/node-ips.txt
  • Solution:
    kubectl get nodes -o jsonpath='{.items[*].status.addresses[?(@.type=="InternalIP")].address}' > /opt/node-ips.txt

TASK 17: Troubleshooting a CrashLoopBackOff Pod (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Pod broken-worker in namespace default in CrashLoopBackOff.
  • Task Description:
    1. Check previous logs of broken-worker to identify the crash reason.
    2. You discover the command sh -c "sleep 10 && exit 1" is failing.
    3. Fix the pod command to sh -c "sleep 3600".
    4. Recreate the pod and verify it stays in Running status.
  • Expected Final Condition: broken-worker is Running with 0 restarts.
  • Verification Command:
    kubectl get pod broken-worker
  • Solution:
    kubectl logs broken-worker --previous
kubectl get pod broken-worker -o yaml > /tmp/broken.yaml
sed -i 's/exit 1/sleep 3600/g' /tmp/broken.yaml
kubectl replace --force -f /tmp/broken.yaml

MOCK EXAM 2: INTERMEDIATE CLUSTER ADMINISTRATION SIMULATION

EXAMINATION SPECIFICATIONS

  • Target Difficulty: Intermediate Administration
  • Time Allowed: 120 minutes (2.0 hours)
  • Passing Score: 66% (Total: 100%)
  • Number of Tasks: 18 Performance-Based Tasks
  • Focus: Cluster lifecycle, kubeadm, storage, traffic management, and workload administration.

TASK 1: Upgrade Control Plane via Kubeadm (Weight: 9%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Primary control plane node cp-node running Kubernetes v1.30.2.
  • Task Description:
    1. Upgrade kubeadm package to version 1.31.2-1.1.
    2. Plan and apply the upgrade using kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2.
    3. Drain cp-node.
    4. Upgrade kubelet and kubectl to 1.31.2-1.1.
    5. Restart kubelet and uncordon cp-node.
  • Expected Final Condition: Control plane node shows version v1.31.2 in Ready status.
  • Solution:
    sudo apt-mark unhold kubeadm
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y kubeadm=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubeadm
sudo kubeadm upgrade plan
sudo kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2 -y
kubectl drain $(hostname) --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data
sudo apt-mark unhold kubelet kubectl
sudo apt-get install -y kubelet=1.31.2-1.1 kubectl=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubelet kubectl
sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart kubelet
kubectl uncordon $(hostname)

TASK 2: Upgrade Worker Node via Kubeadm (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Worker node worker-1 running v1.30.2.
  • Task Description:
    1. Drain worker-1 from the control plane.
    2. SSH to worker-1.
    3. Upgrade kubeadm to 1.31.2-1.1.
    4. Run sudo kubeadm upgrade node.
    5. Upgrade kubelet and kubectl to 1.31.2-1.1.
    6. Restart kubelet and uncordon from control plane.
  • Expected Final Condition: worker-1 runs v1.31.2 in Ready status.
  • Solution:
    kubectl drain worker-1 --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data
ssh worker-1
sudo apt-mark unhold kubeadm
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y kubeadm=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubeadm
sudo kubeadm upgrade node
sudo apt-mark unhold kubelet kubectl
sudo apt-get install -y kubelet=1.31.2-1.1 kubectl=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubelet kubectl
sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart kubelet
exit
kubectl uncordon worker-1

TASK 3: Cluster Certificate Inspection & Renewal (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s

TASK 4: StatefulSet with VolumeClaimTemplates (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace db-prod.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create namespace db-prod.
    2. Create Headless Service mongo-hs on port 27017.
    3. Create StatefulSet mongo with 2 replicas running mongo:6.0.
    4. Configure volumeClaimTemplates requesting 1Gi on storageClassName standard mounted at /data/db.
  • Expected Final Condition: Pods mongo-0 and mongo-1 running with dedicated PVCs.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: StatefulSet
metadata:
  name: mongo
  namespace: db-prod
spec:
  serviceName: mongo-hs
  replicas: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: mongo
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: mongo
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: mongo
        image: mongo:6.0
        volumeMounts:
        - name: data
          mountPath: /data/db
  volumeClaimTemplates:
  - metadata:
      name: data
    spec:
      accessModes: [ "ReadWriteOnce" ]
      storageClassName: standard
      resources:
        requests:
          storage: 1Gi

TASK 7: StorageClass with WaitForFirstConsumer (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s

metadata:
  name: topo-pvc
spec:
  storageClassName: topology-sc
  accessModes: [ "ReadWriteOnce" ]
  resources:
    requests:
      storage: 2Gi

TASK 12: Interlocking ResourceQuota and LimitRange (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace dev-team.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create namespace dev-team.
    2. Create ResourceQuota team-quota with hard.pods: "5" and hard.requests.cpu: "2".
    3. Create LimitRange team-limit with container defaultRequest cpu: 100m and default cpu: 200m.
  • Expected Final Condition: LimitRange injects defaults and satisfies quota automatically.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: team-quota
  namespace: dev-team
spec:
  hard:
    pods: "5"
    requests.cpu: "2"
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: team-limit
  namespace: dev-team
spec:
  limits:
  - default:
      cpu: 200m
    defaultRequest:
      cpu: 100m
    type: Container

MOCK EXAM 3: ADVANCED TROUBLESHOOTING SIMULATION

EXAMINATION SPECIFICATIONS

  • Target Difficulty: Advanced Troubleshooting (High Stress)
  • Time Allowed: 120 minutes (2.0 hours)
  • Passing Score: 66% (Total: 100%)
  • Number of Tasks: 18 Performance-Based Diagnostic Scenarios
  • Focus: Cluster component failure, node outages, broken manifests, storage locks, etcd recovery, and networking drop chains.

TASK 1: Recover Inactive Kubelet Due to Linux Swap (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Worker node worker-1 shows NotReady.
  • Task Description:
    1. SSH to worker-1.
    2. Inspect why systemctl status kubelet reports failed status.
    3. Journalctl indicates swap is active.
    4. Disable swap immediately, disable it in /etc/fstab, and start kubelet.
  • Expected Final Condition: worker-1 returns to Ready status.
  • Solution:
    ssh worker-1
sudo swapoff -a
sudo sed -i '/swap/s/^/#/' /etc/fstab
sudo systemctl restart kubelet
sudo systemctl is-active kubelet
exit
kubectl get nodes worker-1

TASK 2: Kubelet Cgroup Driver Mismatch Recovery (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Worker node worker-2 fails to boot kubelet.
  • Task Description:
    1. SSH to worker-2.
    2. Inspect kubelet journal logs for cgroup error.
    3. Align /var/lib/kubelet/config.yaml to use cgroupDriver: systemd.
    4. Restart kubelet.
  • Expected Final Condition: Kubelet active and node worker-2 shows Ready.
  • Solution:
    ssh worker-2
sudo sed -i 's/cgroupDriver: cgroupfs/cgroupDriver: systemd/g' /var/lib/kubelet/config.yaml
sudo systemctl restart kubelet
exit
kubectl get nodes worker-2

TASK 3: Repair Broken API Server Static Pod Flag (Weight: 7%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: kubectl returns connection refused on port 6443.
  • Task Description:
    1. SSH to control plane node.
    2. Use crictl ps -a to find the exited kube-apiserver container.
    3. View container logs using crictl logs.
    4. Notice error: unknown flag: --etcd-cafile-broken.
    5. Edit /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml and fix flag to --etcd-cafile=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt.
    6. Wait for kubelet to relaunch container.
  • Expected Final Condition: API server boots and kubectl get nodes succeeds.
  • Solution:
    sudo crictl ps -a --name=kube-apiserver
sudo sed -i 's/--etcd-cafile-broken/--etcd-cafile/g' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
sleep 15
kubectl get nodes

TASK 4: Repair Crashed Kube-Scheduler Static Pod (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: All new pods stuck in Pending with 0 scheduler events.
  • Task Description:
    1. Notice kube-scheduler static pod is not running in kube-system.
    2. Inspect /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-scheduler.yaml on control plane.
    3. Fix typo in container command: kube-schedulerr $\to$ kube-scheduler.
  • Expected Final Condition: Scheduler launches and pending pods are scheduled.
  • Solution:
    sudo sed -i 's/kube-schedulerr/kube-scheduler/g' /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-scheduler.yaml

TASK 5: etcd Defragmentation and Space Alarm Disarm (Weight: 6%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: API server returns etcdserver: mvcc: database space exceeded.
  • Task Description:
    1. Compact etcd history to current revision.
    2. Defragment etcd storage.
    3. Disarm NOSPACE alarm.
  • Expected Final Condition: Cluster writes succeed.
  • Solution:
    export ETCDCTL_API=3
export CERTS="--cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.crt --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.key --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379"
REV=$(sudo etcdctl $CERTS endpoint status -w json | jq .[0].Status.header.revision)
sudo etcdctl $CERTS compact $REV
sudo etcdctl $CERTS defrag
sudo etcdctl $CERTS alarm disarm

TASK 6: etcd Point-in-Time Snapshot Restore (Weight: 8%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Backup exists at /opt/backup/etcd.db.
  • Task Description:
    1. Stop static pods by moving manifests out of /etc/kubernetes/manifests/.
    2. Restore snapshot to /var/lib/etcd-recovery.
    3. Update etcd.yaml to point to /var/lib/etcd-recovery.
    4. Move manifests back and verify cluster state.
  • Expected Final Condition: Cluster recovers from snapshot.
  • Solution:
    sudo mv /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml /tmp/
sudo mv /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml /tmp/
sudo ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl --data-dir=/var/lib/etcd-recovery snapshot restore /opt/backup/etcd.db
sudo sed -i 's|/var/lib/etcd|/var/lib/etcd-recovery|g' /tmp/etcd.yaml
sudo mv /tmp/etcd.yaml /etc/kubernetes/manifests/
sudo mv /tmp/kube-apiserver.yaml /etc/kubernetes/manifests/
sleep 15
kubectl get nodes

TASK 7: CoreDNS Loop Crash Recovery (Weight: 5%)

  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: CoreDNS in CrashLoopBackOff with loop error.

    2. Update kubelet config /var/lib/kubelet/config.yaml to set resolvConf: /run/systemd/resolve/resolv.conf.
    3. Restart kubelet and delete crash-looping CoreDNS pods.

sudo systemctl restart kubelet
kubectl delete pods -n kube-system -l k8s-app=kube-dns

TASK 1: Troubleshoot and Repair a NotReady Node (Weight: 6%)

  • Domain: Troubleshooting (30%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Worker node node-2 is in NotReady status.
  • Task Description:
    1. Switch to context k8s.
    2. SSH to node-2.
    3. Identify why kubelet is in auto-restart loop.
    4. Fix configuration and ensure node-2 returns to Ready status.
  • Expected Final Condition: kubectl get nodes shows node-2 in Ready status.
  • Solution:
    kubectl config use-context k8s
ssh node-2
sudo journalctl -u kubelet -n 30 --no-pager
# Identify issue: cgroup driver or swap
sudo swapoff -a
sudo systemctl restart kubelet
exit
kubectl get nodes node-2

TASK 7: Kubeadm Control Plane Upgrade (Weight: 7%)

  • Domain: Cluster Architecture (25%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Primary control plane node at v1.30.2.
  • Task Description:
    1. Upgrade kubeadm to 1.31.2-1.1.
    2. Apply upgrade: kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2.
    3. Drain node, upgrade kubelet and kubectl, restart kubelet, uncordon.
  • Expected Final Condition: Control plane node running v1.31.2.
  • Solution:
    sudo apt-mark unhold kubeadm
sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y kubeadm=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubeadm
sudo kubeadm upgrade plan
sudo kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2 -y
kubectl drain $(hostname) --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data
sudo apt-mark unhold kubelet kubectl
sudo apt-get install -y kubelet=1.31.2-1.1 kubectl=1.31.2-1.1
sudo apt-mark hold kubelet kubectl
sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart kubelet
kubectl uncordon $(hostname)

  • Domain: Cluster Architecture (25%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s

  snapshot save /var/data/etcd-final-mock.db

TASK 9: RBAC Role and RoleBinding with Specific Verbs (Weight: 4%)

  • Domain: Cluster Architecture (25%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace operations.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create ServiceAccount deployer in operations.
    2. Create Role deploy-role allowing create, update, patch on deployments.apps.
    3. Bind Role to ServiceAccount.
  • Expected Final Condition: deployer can mutate deployments in operations.
  • Solution:
    kubectl create namespace operations
kubectl create serviceaccount deployer -n operations
kubectl create role deploy-role -n operations --verb=create,update,patch --resource=deployments.apps
kubectl create rolebinding bind-deployer -n operations --role=deploy-role --serviceaccount=operations:deployer

TASK 12: Gateway API HTTPRoute Traffic Management (Weight: 6%)

  • Domain: Services & Networking (20%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Gateway edge-gw exists in gw-system.
  • Task Description:
    1. In namespace sales, create HTTPRoute named sales-route attached to edge-gw.
    2. Route path /checkout to Service checkout-svc on port 8080.
  • Expected Final Condition: HTTPRoute reports Accepted: True.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: HTTPRoute
metadata:
  name: sales-route
  namespace: sales
spec:
  parentRefs:
  - name: edge-gw
    namespace: gw-system
  rules:
  - matches:
    - path:
        type: PathPrefix
        value: /checkout
    backendRefs:
    - name: checkout-svc
      port: 8080

TASK 13: Ingress with TLS Termination (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Services & Networking (20%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Secret tls-cert exists. Service app-svc on port 80.
  • Task Description:
    1. Author Ingress app-ingress with TLS terminating for app.company.io using Secret tls-cert.
  • Expected Final Condition: Ingress created with TLS configuration.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: Ingress
metadata:
  name: app-ingress
spec:
  ingressClassName: nginx
  tls:
  - hosts:
    - app.company.io
    secretName: tls-cert
  rules:
  - host: app.company.io
    http:
      paths:
      - path: /
        pathType: Prefix
        backend:
          service:
            name: app-svc
            port:
              number: 80

TASK 14: NetworkPolicy Default-Deny with Port 53 Egress (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Services & Networking (20%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace locked-ns.
  • Task Description:
    1. In namespace locked-ns, create NetworkPolicy denying all ingress, and allowing egress strictly on port 53 (UDP/TCP).
  • Expected Final Condition: All ingress dropped; DNS egress permitted.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: lock-and-dns
  namespace: locked-ns
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
  - Ingress
  - Egress
  egress:
  - ports:
    - protocol: UDP
      port: 53
    - protocol: TCP
      port: 53

TASK 17: Pod Anti-Affinity Spreading Across Nodes (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Workloads & Scheduling (15%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Multi-node cluster.
  • Task Description:
    1. Author Deployment web-spread with 2 replicas and podAntiAffinity requiring pods to land on distinct hostnames (kubernetes.io/hostname).
  • Expected Final Condition: Each replica lands on a different worker node.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: web-spread
spec:
  replicas: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: web-spread
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: web-spread
    spec:
      affinity:
        podAntiAffinity:
          requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
          - labelSelector:
              matchExpressions:
              - key: app
                operator: In
                values:
                - web-spread
            topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
      containers:
      - name: nginx
        image: nginx:1.27

TASK 18: ResourceQuota & Container LimitRange Configuration (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Workloads & Scheduling (15%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Namespace staging.
  • Task Description:
    1. Apply ResourceQuota quota-staging with hard.pods: "4".
    2. Apply LimitRange limit-staging with default CPU request 50m and limit 100m.
  • Expected Final Condition: Limits enforced automatically on new pods.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: quota-staging
  namespace: staging
spec:
  hard:
    pods: "4"
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: limit-staging
  namespace: staging
spec:
  limits:
  - default:
      cpu: 100m
    defaultRequest:
      cpu: 50m
    type: Container

TASK 19: PersistentVolume and PersistentVolumeClaim Binding (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Storage (10%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: Directory /mnt/data/vol1 on worker node.
  • Task Description:
    1. Create PV pv-storage-1 of capacity 5Gi, accessMode ReadWriteOnce, storageClassName local-vol, hostPath /mnt/data/vol1.
    2. Create PVC pvc-storage-1 requesting 2Gi on local-vol.
  • Expected Final Condition: PV and PVC are Bound.
  • Solution:
    apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolume
metadata:
  name: pv-storage-1
spec:
  capacity:
    storage: 5Gi
  accessModes: [ "ReadWriteOnce" ]
  storageClassName: local-vol
  hostPath:
    path: /mnt/data/vol1
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolumeClaim
metadata:
  name: pvc-storage-1
spec:
  storageClassName: local-vol
  accessModes: [ "ReadWriteOnce" ]
  resources:
    requests:
      storage: 2Gi

TASK 20: Online PVC Volume Expansion (Weight: 5%)

  • Domain: Storage (10%)
  • Context: kubectl config use-context k8s
  • Initial Condition: PVC expand-claim bound at 1Gi.
  • Task Description:
    1. Dynamically expand expand-claim from 1Gi to 3Gi.
  • Expected Final Condition: PVC capacity shows 3Gi.
  • Solution:
    kubectl patch pvc expand-claim -p '{"spec":{"resources":{"requests":{"storage":"3Gi"}}}}'

CKA READINESS ASSESSMENT & DIAGNOSTIC SYSTEM

INITIAL KNOWLEDGE EVALUATION & PERSONALIZED STUDY ROADMAP

1. ASSESSMENT OBJECTIVE & METHODOLOGY

Before embarking on your CKA preparation journey, taking this 25-question diagnostic assessment establishes your baseline across the 5 official CKA domains.

  • Format: Multiple-choice scenario questions with single best answer.
  • Scoring: 4 points per correct answer (Total: 100 points).
  • Passing Benchmark: 70 points indicates intermediate readiness; below 50 points recommends starting with the 60-Day Beginner Track.

2. DIAGNOSTIC ASSESSMENT QUESTIONS

DOMAIN 1: CLUSTER ARCHITECTURE, INSTALLATION & CONFIGURATION (25%)

Q1. When executing kubeadm init, which flag is mandatory to allow other control plane nodes to securely join an HA cluster?

  • A. --experimental-upload-certs
  • B. --upload-certs
  • C. --ha-enable
  • D. --control-plane-sync

Q2. An etcd cluster consists of 5 members. How many simultaneous member failures can this cluster tolerate while maintaining full write availability?

  • A. 1
  • B. 2
  • C. 3
  • D. 4

Q3. You need to safely upgrade a worker node from v1.31.0 to v1.31.2. Which command must be executed on the control plane before upgrading packages on the worker node?

  • A. kubectl cordon <node>
  • B. kubectl drain <node> --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data
  • C. kubectl delete node <node>
  • D. kubeadm upgrade node

Q4. A ServiceAccount named deployer in namespace qa needs to create deployments in namespace staging. Which RBAC resource must be created?

  • A. Role in qa and RoleBinding in qa.
  • B. ClusterRole and ClusterRoleBinding.
  • C. Role in staging and RoleBinding in staging referencing qa:deployer in subjects.
  • D. Role in staging and RoleBinding in qa.

Q5. Which command is used to renew control plane certificates created by kubeadm?

  • A. kubeadm alpha certs renew
  • B. kubeadm certs renew all
  • C. kubectl cert-manager renew
  • D. openssl req -renew

DOMAIN 2: WORKLOADS & SCHEDULING (15%)

Q6. A Deployment rollout of app-v2 is failing due to CrashLoopBackOff. Which command reverts the deployment to revision 1 without editing YAML?

  • A. kubectl rollout undo deployment app --to-revision=1
  • B. kubectl rollback deployment app -v 1
  • C. kubectl set image deployment app app=app:v1
  • D. kubectl revert deployment app 1

Q7. A container in a Pod is terminated with exit code 137. What is the primary cause?

  • A. Application segmentation fault.
  • B. Memory consumption exceeded resources.limits.memory (OOMKilled).
  • C. Liveness probe failed 3 consecutive times.
  • D. Missing ConfigMap environment variable.

Q8. You want to ensure that Pods of Deployment api are never co-located on the same physical host as another api Pod. Which feature must be declared?

  • A. nodeSelector
  • B. podAntiAffinity with topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
  • C. topologySpreadConstraints with maxSkew: 0
  • D. nodeAffinity with NotIn operator

Q9. A batch Job has completions: 5 and parallelism: 2. How many Pods run simultaneously?

  • A. 1
  • B. 2
  • C. 5
  • D. 10

DOMAIN 3: SERVICES & NETWORKING (20%)

Q10. What is the fully qualified domain name (FQDN) for a Service named auth-svc located in namespace security using default cluster domain?

  • A. auth-svc.security.cluster.local
  • B. auth-svc.security.svc.cluster.local
  • C. auth-svc.svc.security.cluster
  • D. security.auth-svc.k8s.local

Q11. When using the Gateway API, which resource decouples infrastructure listeners (ports and TLS) from developer routing paths?

  • A. Ingress
  • B. GatewayClass
  • C. Gateway
  • D. HTTPRoute

Q12. An HTTPRoute in namespace frontend needs to bind to a Gateway in namespace infra. Which resource must exist in namespace infra to permit this cross-namespace attachment?

  • A. NetworkPolicy
  • B. ReferenceGrant
  • C. ClusterRoleBinding
  • D. SecretExport

Q13. A namespace has a NetworkPolicy with podSelector: {} and policyTypes: [Ingress], but no ingress: rules defined. What is the effect?

  • A. All ingress traffic is allowed.
  • B. All ingress traffic to all pods in the namespace is denied (Default Deny).
  • C. Traffic from the same namespace is allowed, external traffic is denied.
  • D. DNS traffic is blocked.

Q14. In a NetworkPolicy ingress.from clause, what is the semantic difference between listing namespaceSelector and podSelector under a single list item versus two separate hyphens?

  • A. Single item is OR; separate hyphens is AND.
  • B. Single item is AND; separate hyphens is OR.
  • C. Both syntaxes produce identical AND behavior.
  • D. Separate hyphens are invalid syntax.

DOMAIN 4: STORAGE (10%)

Q15. A StorageClass has volumeBindingMode: WaitForFirstConsumer. When is the PersistentVolume dynamically provisioned?

  • A. Immediately when the PersistentVolumeClaim is created.
  • B. When a Pod consuming the PVC is scheduled to a specific node.
  • C. When the first container inside the Pod writes data.
  • D. When the node reboots.

Q16. Which volume access mode guarantees that only a single Pod in the entire cluster can mount the volume read-write?

  • A. ReadWriteOnce (RWO)
  • B. ReadOnlyMany (ROX)
  • C. ReadWriteMany (RWX)
  • D. ReadWriteOncePod (RWOP)

Q17. A PersistentVolume has persistentVolumeReclaimPolicy: Retain. What happens when the bound PersistentVolumeClaim is deleted?

  • A. The PV is immediately deleted and underlying cloud storage destroyed.
  • B. The PV enters Released status; storage and data remain intact on disk.
  • C. The PV enters Available status and binds to any new PVC.
  • D. The PV is formatted with a fresh filesystem.

DOMAIN 5: TROUBLESHOOTING (30%)

Q18. A worker node shows status NotReady. You SSH into the node and run systemctl status kubelet. The service is failed. What is the most effective command to view the crash stack trace?

  • A. kubectl logs kubelet
  • B. journalctl -u kubelet -e --no-pager
  • C. cat /var/log/syslog | grep error
  • D. crictl logs kubelet

Q19. kubectl commands return The connection to the server <ip>:6443 was refused. You SSH into the control plane host. How do you view logs of the crashed API server?

  • A. kubectl logs kube-apiserver -n kube-system
  • B. systemctl status kube-apiserver
  • C. crictl ps -a --name kube-apiserver followed by crictl logs <id>
  • D. tail -f /var/log/messages

Q20. You are executing an etcd snapshot restore on the control plane node. Where should the restored data directory be placed?

  • A. Directly over /var/lib/etcd without flags.
  • B. A brand new directory (e.g. /var/lib/etcd-restored) specified by --data-dir.
  • C. Inside /tmp/etcd/
  • D. Over /etc/kubernetes/pki/

Q21. After restoring etcd to /var/lib/etcd-restored, what must be updated so kubelet launches the restored database?

  • A. kubectl edit pod etcd -n kube-system
  • B. Modify the hostPath.path in /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml.
  • C. kubeadm init phase etcd
  • D. Restart containerd.

Q22. Workload Pods cannot resolve DNS names. CoreDNS pods are in CrashLoopBackOff logging Loop (127.0.0.1:53 -> :53) detected. What is the root cause?

  • A. CoreDNS image is corrupted.
  • B. Host /etc/resolv.conf nameserver points to local loopback (127.0.0.53) causing an infinite forwarding loop.
  • C. Kubelet is missing the --cluster-dns flag.
  • D. CNI plugin is not installed.

Q23. A Pod is stuck in Pending. kubectl describe pod shows 0/3 nodes are available: 3 node(s) had untolerated taint {dedicated: gpu}. What action resolves this?

  • A. Add a matching toleration for dedicated: gpu in the Pod's spec.tolerations.
  • B. Increase the Pod's CPU request.
  • C. Change restartPolicy to Never.
  • D. Re-create the Pod in the kube-system namespace.

Q24. You run kubectl get pods and notice a Pod in CreateContainerConfigError. What is the immediate diagnostic step?

  • A. kubectl logs <pod-name>
  • B. kubectl describe pod <pod-name> to identify missing ConfigMap or Secret keys.
  • C. Restart kubelet.
  • D. Drain the worker node.

Q25. A Service of type ClusterIP has 0 endpoints (ENDPOINTS: <none>). Pods are running. What is the most common cause?

  • A. kube-proxy is stopped.
  • B. Service spec.selector labels do not match the Pod metadata.labels, or Pods are failing readiness probes.
  • C. Node firewall is blocking port 443.
  • D. The Service lacks a cluster-admin RoleBinding.

4. SCORE INTERPRETATION & PERSONALIZED ROADMAP

Score: 88–100 Points (Advanced / Exam Ready)

  • Status: You possess strong theoretical and administrative fundamentals.
  • Recommended Track: 30-Day Accelerated Sprint (Focus on Volume II speed drills, Volume IV JSONPath recipes, and Volume V Mock Exams 3 & 4).

Score: 68–84 Points (Intermediate / Competent)

  • Status: You understand Kubernetes architecture but have blind spots in advanced networking (Gateway API, ReferenceGrant) or Linux-level static pod/etcd troubleshooting.
  • Recommended Track: 45-Day Standard Track (Deep dive into Volume I Parts II, IV, and VI; complete all 67 labs in Volume II).

Score: < 68 Points (Beginner / Foundational)

  • Status: Needs structured foundational study across Linux kernel namespaces, declarative YAML, and core controllers.
  • Recommended Track: 60-Day Comprehensive Track (Start with Volume I Chapters 1–8; complete Volume II beginner labs; follow the progressive schedule).

COMMAND MEMORY SYSTEM & SPACED-REPETITION FLASHCARDS

INSTANT CLI MUSCLE MEMORY FOR CKA EXAM SPEED

1. SPACED-REPETITION METHODOLOGY

Print or review these 24 high-yield flashcards daily. Cover the "Answer / Command" column, read the "Scenario / Challenge", and recite or type the exact syntax without looking.

2. FLASHCARD DECK: IMPERATIVE COMMAND GENERATION

Card # Scenario / Administrative Need Exact Command Syntax Critical Flags & Notes
FC-01 Generate Pod YAML without creating kubectl run web --image=nginx:alpine $do > pod.yaml $do is --dry-run=client -o yaml
FC-02 Create Deployment with 3 replicas on port 80 kubectl create deployment api --image=nginx:alpine --replicas=3 --port=80 Creates apps/v1 Deployment
FC-03 Expose Deployment internally as ClusterIP kubectl expose deployment api --port=80 --target-port=8080 --name=api-svc Inherits deployment label selector
FC-04 Expose Deployment on NodePort 30080 kubectl expose deployment api --type=NodePort --port=80 --target-port=8080 Then edit service to set nodePort: 30080
FC-05 Update image and record revision kubectl set image deployment/api nginx=nginx:1.27
kubectl annotate deployment/api kubernetes.io/change-cause="v1.27" --overwrite		 Replaces removed --record flag!
FC-06 Rollback deployment to specific revision kubectl rollout undo deployment/api --to-revision=2 View history: kubectl rollout history ...
FC-07 Generate Job that runs batch command kubectl create job calc --image=busybox $do -- expr 10 + 20 > job.yaml Must specify -- before container command
FC-08 Generate CronJob running hourly kubectl create cronjob backup --image=busybox --schedule="0 * * * *" $do -- date Uses 5-field cron syntax
FC-09 Create Secret with literal password kubectl create secret generic db-pass --from-literal=password='S3cur3!' Value is Base64 encoded automatically
FC-10 Create TLS Secret with cert and key files kubectl create secret tls web-tls --cert=/path/tls.crt --key=/path/tls.key Creates kubernetes.io/tls type secret
FC-11 Test permissions without switching user kubectl auth can-i create pods -n prod --as=system:serviceaccount:ci:builder Returns yes or no
FC-12 Safely evict pods prior to node maintenance kubectl drain node-01 --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data --force Cordon automatically applied by drain

3. FLASHCARD DECK: CLUSTER ADMINISTRATION & ETCD

Card # Scenario / Administrative Need Exact Command Syntax Critical Flags & Notes
FC-13 Save etcd snapshot to file with mTLS ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379 --cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.crt --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.key snapshot save /opt/snap.db All 3 TLS cert flags are mandatory
FC-14 Verify etcd snapshot integrity ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl --write-out=table snapshot status /opt/snap.db Checks hash, revision, and size
FC-15 Restore etcd snapshot to new directory ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl snapshot restore /opt/snap.db --data-dir=/var/lib/etcd-new --initial-cluster="m=https://127.0.0.1:2380" --initial-cluster-token="t" --name="m" Never restore directly over /var/lib/etcd
FC-16 Check kubeadm certificate expiration sudo kubeadm certs check-expiration Reports residual time for all PKI certs
FC-17 Renew all control plane certificates sudo kubeadm certs renew all Remember to copy admin.conf to ~/.kube/config!
FC-18 Upgrade kubeadm control plane sudo kubeadm upgrade plan
sudo kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2 -y		 Control plane must be upgraded before workers

4. FLASHCARD DECK: JSONPATH, TROUBLESHOOTING & LINUX

Card # Scenario / Administrative Need Exact Command Syntax Critical Flags & Notes
FC-19 Sort all pods by creation timestamp kubectl get pods -A --sort-by=.metadata.creationTimestamp Instant view of newly restarted pods
FC-20 Extract Pod IPs in a single tab-separated line kubectl get pods -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{"\t"}{.status.podIP}{"\n"}{end}' JSONPath range loop syntax
FC-21 List static containers running on host sudo crictl ps -a --name kube-apiserver Primary tool when apiserver is down
FC-22 View kubelet systemd journal crash log sudo journalctl -u kubelet -n 50 --no-pager Bypasses pager for instant inspection
FC-23 Test TCP port connectivity with 3s timeout nc -zvw3 192.168.1.10 6443 Tests if port is open and listening
FC-24 Instant temporary debug pod for DNS kubectl run net-test --rm -it --image=busybox:1.28 -- nslookup kubernetes.default --rm automatically cleans up pod

TROUBLESHOOTING DECISION TREES CATALOG

THE 6 COMPREHENSIVE INCIDENT RESPONSE PROCEDURES

1. INCIDENT TREE 1: NODE NOTREADY RECOVERY

Symptom: Node status reports NotReady in 'kubectl get nodes'
  │
  ├── 1. Initial Check: kubectl describe node <node> | grep -A 5 Conditions
  │     ├── DiskPressure = True ──> Clean disk: sudo crictl rmi --prune
  │     ├── MemoryPressure = True ──> Identify OOM leak, kill rogue containers
  │     └── Ready = False / Unknown ──> Node agent (kubelet) communication severed
  │
  ├── 2. SSH to Node & Inspect Service:
  │     sudo systemctl status kubelet
  │     ├── Active: inactive (dead) ──> sudo systemctl restart kubelet
  │     └── Active: failed (Result: exit-code) ──> sudo journalctl -u kubelet -e --no-pager
  │
  ├── 3. Analyze Kubelet Failure Reason:
  │     ├── "swap is enabled" ──> sudo swapoff -a && sed -i '/swap/d' /etc/fstab
  │     ├── "cgroup driver mismatch" ──> Set SystemdCgroup = true in /etc/containerd/config.toml
  │     ├── "connection refused to 6443" ──> Control plane unreachable; check firewall/routing
  │     └── "certificate has expired" ──> Renew /var/lib/kubelet/pki/ certs via bootstrap
  │
  └── 4. Verification:
        sudo systemctl restart containerd && sudo systemctl restart kubelet
        kubectl get nodes <node> (Transitions to Ready within 30 seconds)

2. INCIDENT TREE 2: CONTROL PLANE APISERVER DOWN (PORT 6443 REFUSED)

Symptom: kubectl returns "The connection to server <ip>:6443 was refused"
  │
  ├── 1. SSH to Control Plane Host:
  │     sudo systemctl is-active kubelet
  │     └── If inactive ──> sudo systemctl restart kubelet
  │
  ├── 2. Inspect Static Pod Containers via Runtime:
  │     sudo crictl ps -a --name kube-apiserver
  │     ├── Container is Exited / Crashing ──> sudo crictl logs <container-id>
  │     └── Container is Missing ──> Verify /etc/kubernetes/manifests/kube-apiserver.yaml
  │
  ├── 3. Identify Root Error from Logs:
  │     ├── "etcdserver: request timed out" ──> etcd is dead on port 2379; check etcd container
  │     ├── "unknown flag: --xxx" ──> Typo in static pod manifest args; fix YAML syntax
  │     └── "tls: bad certificate" ──> Expired PKI cert; run: sudo kubeadm certs renew all
  │
  └── 4. Verification:
        curl -k https://127.0.0.1:6443/healthz (Returns "ok")
        kubectl get nodes (Restores full CLI operation)

3. INCIDENT TREE 3: POD CRASHLOOPBACKOFF & EXIT CODE ANALYSIS

Symptom: Pod continuously crashes; restartCount increases rapidly
  │
  ├── 1. Fetch Previous Crash Log:
  │     kubectl logs <pod-name> -n <ns> --previous
  │     └── If empty ──> kubectl logs <pod-name> -n <ns>
  │
  ├── 2. Inspect Last State & Termination Reason:
  │     kubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n <ns>
  │     ├── Exit Code 0 ──> Container finished work; add long-running daemon / sleep
  │     ├── Exit Code 1 ──> Application exception, syntax error, or unhandled promise
  │     ├── Exit Code 137 ──> OOMKilled; increase resources.limits.memory in pod spec
  │     ├── Exit Code 139 ──> Segmentation fault in compiled binary
  │     ├── Exit Code 143 ──> Gracefully terminated by external SIGTERM
  │     └── Exit Code 255 ──> Entrypoint / CMD executable not found in image
  │
  └── 3. Verification:
        kubectl get pod <pod-name> -n <ns> (Status remains Running for >60 seconds)

4. INCIDENT TREE 4: SERVICE CONNECTIVITY & MISSING ENDPOINTS

Symptom: curl http://<service-ip>:<port> drops packets or times out
  │
  ├── 1. Inspect Endpoints:
  │     kubectl get endpoints <service-name> -n <ns>
  │     │
  │     ├── If ENDPOINTS: <none>
  │     │   ├── Check Labels: Compare service spec.selector with pod metadata.labels
  │     │   └── Check Readiness: Are pods passing readiness probes? (Unready = Stripped)
  │     │
  │     └── If ENDPOINTS: Populated with Pod IPs
  │         ├── Direct Connect: Test curl http://<pod-ip>:<targetPort> from another pod
  │         │   ├── If Direct Pod Fails ──> Container app not listening on targetPort
  │         │   └── If Direct Pod Works ──> kube-proxy iptables rule programming failed
  │         │
  │         └── Check NetworkPolicies:
  │             kubectl get netpol -n <ns> (Look for policies dropping ingress traffic)
  │
  └── 2. Verification:
        kubectl run curl-test --rm -it --image=curlimages/curl -- curl -Iv http://<service-name>.<ns>

5. INCIDENT TREE 5: COREDNS NAME RESOLUTION OUTAGE

Symptom: Pods cannot resolve service names (e.g. "nslookup kubernetes.default" fails)
  │
  ├── 1. Inspect CoreDNS Pods in kube-system:
  │     kubectl get pods -n kube-system -l k8s-app=kube-dns
  │     ├── Status: CrashLoopBackOff ──> Check logs: kubectl logs -n kube-system -l k8s-app=kube-dns
  │     │   └── "Loop detected" ──> Host /etc/resolv.conf points to 127.0.0.53; fix host DNS
  │     │
  │     └── Status: Running ──> Inspect CoreDNS Service
  │         kubectl get svc kube-dns -n kube-system (Verify ClusterIP: 10.96.0.10)
  │         kubectl get endpoints kube-dns -n kube-system (Verify endpoints exist)
  │
  ├── 2. Test CoreDNS Direct Query from Debug Pod:
  │     kubectl run dns-test --rm -it --image=busybox:1.28 -- nslookup 10.96.0.1 10.96.0.10
  │     ├── Succeeds on IP, fails on names ──> /etc/resolv.conf search path missing in pod
  │     └── Timeouts completely ──> NetworkPolicy in caller namespace blocks Port 53 UDP
  │
  └── 3. Verification:
        kubectl run dns-test --rm -it --image=busybox:1.28 -- nslookup kubernetes.default

6. INCIDENT TREE 6: PERSISTENTVOLUMECLAIM BINDING FAILURE

Symptom: PersistentVolumeClaim remains in Pending status indefinitely
  │
  ├── 1. Inspect PVC Events:
  │     kubectl describe pvc <pvc-name> -n <ns>
  │     │
  │     ├── "waiting for first consumer to be created"
  │     │   └── NORMAL BEHAVIOR: StorageClass uses volumeBindingMode: WaitForFirstConsumer.
  │     │       PVC will bind automatically once a Pod referencing it is scheduled!
  │     │
  │     ├── "no persistent volumes available for this claim"
  │     │   ├── Capacity Mismatch: Existing PV capacity < requested PVC storage
  │     │   ├── AccessMode Mismatch: PV accessModes do not include PVC requested mode
  │     │   └── StorageClass Mismatch: PV storageClassName != PVC storageClassName
  │     │
  │     └── "provisioning failed"
  │         └── Dynamic Provisioning Error: CSI driver down or cloud API IAM denied
  │
  └── 2. Verification:
        kubectl get pvc <pvc-name> -n <ns> (Status transitions from Pending to Bound)

CKA CANDIDATE MISTAKE JOURNAL & POST-MORTEM SYSTEM

STRUCTURED ROOT-CAUSE AUDITING TO PREVENT EXAM REPEAT ERRORS

1. THE MISTAKE JOURNAL PROTOCOL

Research proves that learners who document and categorize their coding mistakes retain correct procedures 3x longer than those who simply read the solution.

Whenever you fail a lab or mock exam task, complete this 5-point root-cause template:

  1. Error Event: Exactly what command was run and what error output was returned.
  2. Category of Error: Syntax typo, namespace oversight, context mismatch, conceptual misconception, or documentation inability.
  3. True Root Cause: Why the error occurred mechanically inside Kubernetes.
  4. Corrective Procedure: The exact verified command sequence that permanently fixes it.
  5. Prevention Rule: A one-sentence rule you will recite to never make this mistake again.

2. COMMON HIGH-FREQUENCY MISTAKE REGISTER

Mistake 1: The Context Amnesia Error

  • Symptom: Task asks to fix node in cluster infra-cluster, but candidate executes commands against k8s. Result: 0 points awarded on official exam.
  • Root Cause: Failing to run the context switch command specified at the top of the question.
  • Corrective Procedure:
    kubectl config use-context infra-cluster
kubectl config current-context # Verify
  • Prevention Rule: Never type a single kubectl command without first copying and executing the task's context switch line.

Mistake 2: The Deprecated Flag Trap

  • Symptom: Running kubectl set image deployment/app nginx=nginx:1.27 --record produces warnings or fails to record revision history.
  • Root Cause: --record was permanently removed in kubectl v1.26+.
  • Corrective Procedure:
    kubectl set image deployment/app nginx=nginx:1.27
kubectl annotate deployment/app kubernetes.io/change-cause="Upgrade to 1.27" --overwrite
  • Prevention Rule: Always use kubernetes.io/change-cause annotations to record deployment history.

Mistake 3: The etcd Direct Overwrite Disaster

  • Symptom: Executing etcdctl snapshot restore /opt/backup.db directly restores over /var/lib/etcd, causing corrupted Raft logs and an unbootable control plane.
  • Root Cause: Omitting --data-dir=/var/lib/etcd-restored forces etcd to write into the active database path.
  • Corrective Procedure:
    ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl snapshot restore /opt/backup.db \
  --data-dir=/var/lib/etcd-restored \
  --initial-cluster="master=https://127.0.0.1:2380" \
  --name="master"
# Update /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml hostPath to /var/lib/etcd-restored
  • Prevention Rule: Always restore etcd snapshots to a brand new target directory and point the static pod manifest to that new path.

Mistake 4: The NetworkPolicy Egress DNS Lockout

  • Symptom: You apply an egress NetworkPolicy to isolate a pod. Suddenly, the pod throws Name or service not known and cannot communicate with any internal service.
  • Root Cause: Applying any egress policy turns on default-deny for all outbound traffic, silently blocking UDP Port 53 queries to CoreDNS.
  • Corrective Procedure: Always append an explicit DNS egress rule to every egress NetworkPolicy:
    egress:
- ports:
  - protocol: UDP
    port: 53
  - protocol: TCP
    port: 53
  • Prevention Rule: Every egress NetworkPolicy must explicitly whitelist UDP port 53 to allow CoreDNS resolution.

3. BLANK MISTAKE JOURNAL TEMPLATE FOR CANDIDATE USE

### MISTAKE ENTRY #[NUMBER]
* **Date & Lab/Mock Task:** [e.g., Day 12 / Vol II Lab 42]
* **Target Objective:** [e.g., Restricting ingress traffic to backend pods]
* **What I Typed / Ran:**
  ```bash
  [Insert erroneous command or manifest snippet here]
  • What Kubernetes Returned:
    [Insert error message, HTTP status code, or unexpected behavior]
  • Why It Failed Mechanically: [Explain the internal reason: e.g., Missing hyphen in YAML created AND instead of OR]
  • The Correct Solution:
    [Insert verified working command]
  • My Prevention Rule: "[State your memorable takeaway rule]"

## 2. TOP 10 PRODUCTION INTERVIEW QUESTIONS & ARCHITECTURAL ANSWERS

### Q1: "Walk me through what happens when an engineer runs `kubectl apply -f deployment.yaml` until the pod is serving traffic."
**Expected Senior Answer:**
1. **Client & API Layer:** `kubectl` performs client-side validation, sends an HTTPS POST/PUT to `kube-apiserver`.
2. **Authentication & Authorization:** The API server authenticates the client certificate/token, then checks RBAC rules via the Authorizer.
3. **Admission Controllers:** Mutating admission webhooks run (injecting defaults, sidecars), followed by OpenAPI schema validation and validating admission webhooks.
4. **etcd Write:** The Deployment object is serialized and committed to `etcd` via Raft consensus.
5. **Deployment Controller:** The Deployment Controller detects the new object via watch streams, computes the replica diff, and creates a `ReplicaSet`.
6. **ReplicaSet Controller:** Detects the ReplicaSet and creates unassigned Pod specifications (with `spec.nodeName: ""`).
7. **kube-scheduler:** Scheduler filter plugins evaluate node capacity, taints, and affinity. Scoring plugins rank passing nodes; scheduler writes a `/binding` subresource assigning the Pod to a node.
8. **Kubelet SyncLoop:** The kubelet on the target node receives the pod assignment via watch stream.
9. **CRI Runtime Call:** Kubelet calls `containerd` via CRI gRPC. Containerd requests `runc` to configure Linux namespaces (PID, net, mnt) and cgroups.
10. **CNI Network Setup:** The CNI plugin creates a `veth` pair, connects one end to the container netns, joins the other to the node bridge/overlay, and allocates a Pod IP from IPAM.
11. **Storage Mounts:** VolumeManager calls CSI plugins to attach and mount required volumes.
12. **Container Launch & Probes:** The application container starts. Kubelet runs startup and readiness probes.
13. **Endpoint Injection:** The EndpointSlice controller detects the ready pod, adds its IP to the Service EndpointSlice, and `kube-proxy` programs local `iptables`/IPVS rules. Traffic flows!

---

### Q2: "A production node suddenly transitions to `NotReady`. How do you triage it under a high-severity outage without causing further downtime?"
**Expected Senior Answer:**
1. **Assess Impact & PDBs:** First check `kubectl get pods -o wide --field-selector spec.nodeName=<node>` to see which workloads are affected. Verify PodDisruptionBudgets so emergency evictions don't breach SLAs.
2. **Examine Conditions:** `kubectl describe node <node>`. Check `DiskPressure`, `MemoryPressure`, `PIDPressure`.
3. **SSH Triage:**
   * Run `systemctl is-active kubelet` and `systemctl is-active containerd`.
   * Check kernel dmesg: `dmesg -T | grep -E "oom|killed|error"`.
   * Check journal logs: `journalctl -u kubelet -n 100 --no-pager`.
4. **Network Verification:** Test if the node can reach the API server: `curl -k https://<cp-ip>:6443/healthz`.
5. **Safe Resolution:**
   * If swap was accidentally turned on after reboot: `swapoff -a && systemctl restart kubelet`.
   * If disk full: run `crictl rmi --prune` and clear dead container logs under `/var/log/pods/`.
   * If hardware failure: run `kubectl drain <node> --ignore-daemonsets --delete-emptydir-data` to reschedule pods gracefully onto healthy nodes, then terminate the VM.

---

### Q3: "What are the mechanical differences between `iptables` mode and `IPVS` mode in `kube-proxy`, and when would you switch?"
**Expected Senior Answer:**
* **iptables:** Operates as a flat, sequential list of packet filter rules. Every service and endpoint adds multiple chains (`KUBE-SERVICES`, `KUBE-SVC-XXX`, `KUBE-SEP-XXX`). Rule evaluation complexity is $O(N)$. In clusters with >5,000 services, sequential rule processing causes noticeable latency, and iptables sync loops consume significant kernel CPU.
* **IPVS (IP Virtual Server):** Implements transport-layer load balancing inside the Linux kernel using hash tables with $O(1)$ lookup complexity. It supports multiple scheduling algorithms (Round Robin, Least Connection, Destination/Source Hashing).
* **When to switch:** Switch to IPVS in clusters scaling beyond 1,000 services or when connection rebalancing (e.g., distributing high-throughput microservices) is required.

---

### Q4: "How does the Raft consensus algorithm work in etcd, and why does an etcd cluster always require an odd number of members?"
**Expected Senior Answer:**
* etcd uses Raft to maintain a replicated, consistent state machine across distributed nodes.
* Operations require a strict quorum: $\lfloor N/2 \rfloor + 1$ active members.
* **Why odd numbers:**
  * A 3-node cluster requires 2 votes for quorum; it tolerates 1 failure ($3 - 2 = 1$).
  * A 4-node cluster requires 3 votes for quorum; it *still* only tolerates 1 failure ($4 - 3 = 1$). Adding a 4th node increases network replication overhead without increasing fault tolerance!
  * A 5-node cluster requires 3 votes for quorum; it tolerates 2 failures ($5 - 3 = 2$).
  * Therefore, odd numbers ($3, 5$) maximize fault tolerance per node cost and avoid split-brain split votes during leader election.

---

### Q5: "How does Kubernetes enforce CPU limits vs. Memory limits at the Linux kernel level?"
**Expected Senior Answer:**
* **CPU Limits:** Enforced via the Linux kernel **Completely Fair Scheduler (CFS) bandwidth control** (`cpu.cfs_quota_us` and `cpu.cfs_period_us`). If a container exceeds its quota within a 100ms period, the kernel throttles its execution cycles. The process is paused until the next CFS period; it is **never killed**.
* **Memory Limits:** Enforced via **memory cgroup ceilings** (`memory.max` in cgroups v2). When a container process allocates memory exceeding the limit and the host cannot reclaim cached pages, the Linux kernel invokes the **Out-Of-Memory (OOM) Killer**, terminating the process immediately with SIGKILL (Exit Code 137).

## 2. ONE-PAGE DOMAIN CHEAT SHEETS

### DOMAIN 1: CLUSTER ARCHITECTURE & CONFIGURATION
```bash
# 1. Kubeadm Upgrade Sequence
sudo apt-get install -y kubeadm=1.31.2-1.1
sudo kubeadm upgrade plan
sudo kubeadm upgrade apply v1.31.2 -y
kubectl drain <node> --ignore-daemonsets
sudo apt-get install -y kubelet=1.31.2-1.1 kubectl=1.31.2-1.1
sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart kubelet
kubectl uncordon <node>

# 2. etcd Backup & Restore
ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl --endpoints=https://127.0.0.1:2379 \
  --cacert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/ca.crt --cert=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.crt \
  --key=/etc/kubernetes/pki/etcd/server.key snapshot save /opt/snap.db

ETCDCTL_API=3 etcdctl snapshot restore /opt/snap.db --data-dir=/var/lib/etcd-new \
  --initial-cluster="m=https://127.0.0.1:2380" --name="m"
# Update /etc/kubernetes/manifests/etcd.yaml hostPath to /var/lib/etcd-new

DOMAIN 2: WORKLOADS & SCHEDULING

# 1. Rolling Updates & Revision Recording
kubectl set image deployment/app nginx=nginx:1.27
kubectl annotate deployment/app kubernetes.io/change-cause="v1.27 upgrade" --overwrite
kubectl rollout undo deployment/app --to-revision=1

# 2. Key Pod Scheduling Fields
spec.nodeSelector: { disk: ssd }
spec.affinity.nodeAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution.nodeSelectorTerms...
spec.tolerations: [{ key: "dedicated", operator: "Equal", value: "gpu", effect: "NoSchedule" }]

DOMAIN 3: SERVICES & GATEWAY API

# Gateway API HTTPRoute Skeleton
apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: HTTPRoute
metadata:
  name: api-route
  namespace: apps
spec:
  parentRefs:
  - name: shared-gw
    namespace: infra
  rules:
  - matches:
    - path: { type: PathPrefix, value: /api }
    backendRefs:
    - name: api-v1
      port: 8080
      weight: 90
    - name: api-v2
      port: 8080
      weight: 10

DOMAIN 4: STORAGE & DYNAMIC PROVISIONING

# WaitForFirstConsumer StorageClass & Claim
apiVersion: storage.k8s.io/v1
kind: StorageClass
metadata: { name: local-sc }
provisioner: kubernetes.io/no-provisioner
volumeBindingMode: WaitForFirstConsumer
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolumeClaim
metadata: { name: db-pvc }
spec:
  storageClassName: local-sc
  accessModes: [ReadWriteOncePod]
  resources: { requests: { storage: 10Gi } }
